Dear Members of the Selection Committee,
Springfield Technical Community College carries the legacy of the Springfield Armory into a different kind of opportunity — opening doors in manufacturing, STEM, healthcare, business, and the liberal arts for students who have too often faced barriers to higher education. As the Commonwealth’s only technical community college and a designated Hispanic Serving Institution, STCC measures success not only in applications, but in enrollment, retention, and completion. That is the work we want to help you grow.
Propellant Media is a higher education enrollment marketing agency. We build data-driven annual marketing plans, place and manage media across digital and traditional channels, and report results against the metrics that matter to a community college: cost per qualified inquiry, application volume, and ultimately enrolled students. We have done this work for institutions that look like STCC — community colleges, Hispanic Serving Institutions, and workforce-focused programs — and we would bring that same playbook to Western Massachusetts.
We recorded a short Loom video walking through this proposal and our recommendations for STCC. You can view it here:
Video Proposal Can Be Found Here
Why Propellant Media
•  Community college and HSI specialists — We grew enrollment 63% and generated
1,381 leads in 30 days for a Hispanic-serving community college using the same channels STCC needs.
•  Google Premier Partner — Top 3% of agencies in the United States, with the media-buying and analytics depth that ranking certifies.
•  Federally certified MBE (NMSDC) — Our certification aligns with STCC’s mission of closing achievement gaps and serving a diverse student population.
•  Live Google Analytics dashboards — You see campaign and traffic data in real time, synthesized into monthly reports your committee can act on.
•  Workforce and adult-learner targeting — We build conquest and re-engagement campaigns that reach the working adults and career-changers your workforce programs depend on.
•  10+ years and a full-service team — Founded in 2015, with graphic design, research, creative, digital, audio, and video staff under one roof.
We have reviewed RFP 2026-22 in full and submit this Technical Proposal in compliance with its requirements. Our Price Proposal is submitted separately by email as instructed, with no pricing contained in this document. One clarification on a required form is noted in our Required Forms appendix. Propellant Media certifies that no corporate officers or key personnel assigned to this engagement have a criminal history related to fiscal matters, in compliance with the minimum requirements set forth in RFP 2026-22 Section II.
Finally, below is a sample creative our team developed including banner assets.
Authorized contact and signatory: Justin Croxton, Chief Executive Officer, Propellant Media, LLC, 800 Battery Avenue SE, Suite 300, Atlanta, GA 30339. Phone: 1 (877) 776-7358.
We would welcome the opportunity to partner with STCC. Thank you for your consideration.
Sincerely,
Justin Croxton
Chief Executive Officer

Executive Summary
STCC is looking for a partner that can build a data-informed annual marketing plan, place and manage media across multiple channels, and prove what is working — every month, in language your committee and cabinet can use. This is the core of what we do. The sections that follow map directly to your scope of services and the five criteria your selection committee will score.
STCC’s situation is specific. You are an affordable, mission-driven technical community college and a Hispanic Serving Institution competing for students across Western Massachusetts against four-year transfer options, other community colleges, and the pull of the labor market itself. Your Momentum: 2023–2028 Strategic Plan ties marketing to enrollment, retention, and completion. A generic higher-ed campaign will not move those numbers. A campaign built around adult learners, workforce and CTE pathways, bilingual creative, and tight geographic targeting will.
Here is what we heard in RFP 2026-22, and how we respond to each:

Propellant Media Overview & History of Propellant Media
Founded: 2015. Headquarters: 800 Battery Avenue SE, Suite 300, Atlanta, GA 30339, with a second office in Charlotte, NC. Phone: 1 (877) 776-7358.
Propellant Media is a full-service digital marketing and media-buying agency specializing in higher education enrollment marketing. We have been providing marketing and advertising services for more than ten years, satisfying STCC’s minimum experience requirement. Our team includes graphic design, research, creative, digital, audio, and video staff in-house — well in excess of the ten-employee minimum required in Section VI.
Credentials
•  Google Premier Partner — Top 3% of agencies in the United States.
•  Federally certified Minority Business Enterprise — Certified through the NMSDC.
•  Higher education focus — Community colleges, Hispanic Serving Institutions, public and private universities, and workforce programs.
•  Full-service media — Google Ads, Meta, LinkedIn, YouTube, programmatic display, geofencing, analytics, creative, and landing page optimization.

The office servicing the STCC account is our Atlanta headquarters, supported by our Charlotte team.
Your Account Team & Our Expertise
STCC’s account is staffed by a named team with direct higher education experience. Your assigned account manager, Alyssa Dixon, has well over the five years of marketing and advertising experience required in Section VI.

Data-Driven Strategic Planning & Campaign Development
This section addresses the selection criterion weighted highest by STCC: the ability to collaborate with the College to produce and execute effective, data-driven marketing campaigns across various media. Our planning process is built to map to Momentum: 2023–2028 and to be reassessed every month, exactly as Section V requires.
Our Planning Process
•  Discovery and goal mapping — We start with your Momentum strategic plan, enrollment targets by program, and the audiences each program needs to reach.
•  Audience definition — Traditional-age prospects, transfer students, adult learners, and workforce/CTE career-changers, segmented and prioritized by program goals.
•  Channel and budget allocation — Funds allocated across channels based on where each audience actually is, then rebalanced monthly on performance.
•  Monthly assessment — Every month we review effectiveness, document what changed, and adjust the plan. This is the cadence Section V calls for.

When a channel underperforms for a given program, the budget shifts the following month. Nothing stays funded out of habit.
Creative Process & Ad Design Samples
This section addresses STCC’s second 25% criterion: experience with creative content development and deployment of marketing material across multiple digital platforms. Creative is where a community college campaign earns attention — or gets scrolled past.
Our Creative Philosophy
Community college creative has to do two things at once: feel attainable and feel like a real path to a career. We build creative that shows students like STCC’s — working adults, first-generation students, career-changers — and ties each ad to a concrete program outcome. For an HSI audience, that means English and Spanish creative built from the start, not translated as an afterthought.
Creative Process
•  Creative brief — Built from the program goal, audience, and message for each campaign.
•  Concept and design — Static and video concepts produced in-house by our creative team under Diana Woodruff.
•  Accessibility check — Every asset verified against WCAG 2.1 Level AA before launch.
•  Performance rotation — Multiple variations launched, then rotated toward the top performers based on engagement and conversion data.

Emerging Advertising Trends in the Community College Market
This section addresses STCC’s third 25% criterion: knowledge of emerging advertising trends in the community college setting. This is where community college marketing differs most from four-year university marketing, and where a generic agency falls short.
What Is Changing in Community College Enrollment Marketing
•  Adult learners are the growth audience — Working adults and career-changers now drive much of community college enrollment. They respond to outcome-first messaging and flexible scheduling, reached through search and conquest targeting rather than traditional college-bound channels.
•  Workforce and CTE pathways lead — Programs tied to local employer demand (healthcare, manufacturing, skilled trades) convert best when ads name the job, the wage, and the timeline, not just the credential.
•  Bilingual and equity-aligned creative — For HSIs like STCC, Spanish-language creative and culturally relevant messaging materially lift engagement among Hispanic prospects and their families.
•  Short-form video dominates reach — Reels, Shorts, and TikTok-style vertical video are now where younger prospects spend attention. We deploy these only where they fit the audience and only with creative built for the format.
AI-Powered Strategies We Apply
STCC’s scope is data-driven, and the tools that make data actionable are increasingly AI-assisted. We apply these in a grounded way, not as buzzwords:
•  Predictive audience modeling — We build lookalike and predictive audiences from your best-performing inquiries to find more students like the ones who actually enroll.
•  Dynamic Creative Optimization — Ad components are tested and recombined automatically toward the highest-performing version per audience segment.
•  Performance Max — Google’s AI-driven campaign type, used to extend reach across Search, Display, YouTube, and Discover from a single program goal.
•  Anonymous visitor identification — Identifying and re-engaging high-intent site visitors who did not inquire on first visit.

Student-facing communication is always reviewed by our team — automation supports the work, it does not replace judgment.
Media Buying & Media Plan
STCC needs to reach several different audiences across Western Massachusetts — traditional-age students, transfer prospects, working adults, and career-changers feeding your workforce programs — on a budget that has to work hard. Our approach is to build one integrated plan that allocates spend by audience and program priority, then reassess it every month against cost per qualified inquiry and application volume. Nothing in the plan is fixed for the year; it moves with the data.
Our media philosophy is full-funnel. Awareness channels build recognition across the Pioneer Valley, consideration channels capture students actively researching programs, and conversion and yield channels move inquiries to applications and applicants to enrolled students. We do not buy reach for its own sake — every channel has a job in the funnel and a metric attached to it.
For STCC’s audience and geography, the strongest mix concentrates on Google Search (capturing high-intent program seekers), Meta (reaching adult learners and families), programmatic display and geofencing (targeting feeder high schools and competitor campuses), and YouTube (affordable video reach). Traditional placements are layered in where they reach specific local audiences. Because the RFP does not state a media budget, this plan presents channel strategy as percentage allocations that scale to whatever budget STCC sets; all media-spend and fee dollar figures appear only in our separately submitted Price Proposal.

Disclaimer: Percentages above illustrate strategy only. All fee and media-spend dollar figures are contained in the separately submitted Price Proposal, per RFP Section VI.
Campaign Cycle Timing
•  Fall push (Apr–Aug) — Heaviest spend ahead of fall enrollment, with adult-learner and workforce emphasis.
•  Spring push (Oct–Dec) — Secondary enrollment cycle, transfer and adult re-engagement.
<w:pgSz w:h="15840" w:w="12240" w:orient="portrait"/><w:pgMar w:bottom="1440" w:top="1440" w:left="1440" w:right="1440" w:header="708" w:footer="708"/></w:sectPr></w:pPr><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:b w:val="0"/><w:bCs w:val="0"/><w:i w:val="0"/><w:iCs w:val="0"/><w:color w:val="000000"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">•  Always-on search — Google Search runs year-round to capture intent whenever it appears.
CRM Integration & Campaign Attribution
STCC’s scope centers on data — and the data that matters most lives in your system of record, where inquiries become applicants and applicants become enrolled students. Connecting our media data to that system is how we prove which campaigns actually produce enrolled students, not just clicks.
Segmented Audiences & Predictive Targeting
•  Inquiry-based audiences — We build targeting from your real inquiry and applicant records to find more students like the ones who enroll.
•  Stage-based messaging — Prospects, inquiries, applicants, and admits each receive different creative matched to their funnel stage.

Our Three-Step Framework
•  1. Pixel-to-Ping Data Connection — Tracking pixels and UTM parameters map every ad interaction to a source, feeding your system of record automatically.
•  2. Audience Mirroring — Your best-performing inquiry and applicant segments are mirrored into ad platforms as targeting audiences.
•  3. Application Yield Reporting — Reporting closes the loop from ad spend to enrolled student, so budget follows what actually yields.

Custom Analytics, Dashboards & Reporting
This section addresses STCC’s fifth criterion: knowledge of KPIs and data analytics through effective reporting. Section V specifically calls for comprehensive Google Analytics dashboards with live data and meaningful reports — this is built into how we work, not an add-on.

Website & Landing Page Optimization
Paid media drives traffic; landing pages decide whether that traffic becomes an inquiry. For a community college audience that often researches on mobile and on limited time, the gap between a good ad and a converting page is where budget is won or lost.
What We Optimize
•  Clear, single calls to action — Each campaign points to a focused page with one obvious next step (request info, apply, attend an info session).
•  Short, mobile-first forms — We test form length to reduce friction for adult learners completing inquiries on a phone.
•  Page speed — Slow pages lose mobile prospects; we flag and help resolve speed issues.
•  Accessibility — Landing pages built and tested to WCAG 2.1 Level AA, consistent with Section V.
<w:pgSz w:h="15840" w:w="12240" w:orient="portrait"/><w:pgMar w:bottom="1440" w:top="1440" w:left="1440" w:right="1440" w:header="708" w:footer="708"/></w:sectPr></w:pPr><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:b w:val="0"/><w:bCs w:val="0"/><w:i w:val="0"/><w:iCs w:val="0"/><w:color w:val="000000"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">We work with STCC’s web team to recommend and test improvements; we do not require control of your CMS to drive conversion gains.
The Competitive Landscape for Springfield Technical Community College
STCC competes for students in a crowded Western Massachusetts market — against other community colleges, four-year transfer options, for-profit and online providers, and the labor market pulling working adults toward jobs rather than classrooms. Winning enrollment means owning the searches and audiences your competitors underinvest in.
Market Context
•  Regional competition — Holyoke Community College, Greenfield Community College, and area four-year institutions compete for the same Pioneer Valley prospects.
•  Transfer pull — UMass Amherst and the state universities draw transfer-minded students who could start at STCC.
•  Workforce opportunity — STCC’s technical and healthcare programs map to real regional employer demand, a story competitors tell less effectively.
SWOT Analysis
<w:tblPr><w:tblStyle w:val="Table18"/><w:tblW w:w="9360.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:jc w:val="left"/><w:tblBorders><w:top w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="4" w:val="single"/><w:left w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="4" w:val="single"/><w:bottom w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="4" w:val="single"/><w:right w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="4" w:val="single"/><w:insideH w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="4" w:val="single"/><w:insideV w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="4" w:val="single"/></w:tblBorders><w:tblLayout w:type="fixed"/><w:tblLook w:val="0000"/></w:tblPr><w:tblGrid><w:gridCol w:w="4680"/><w:gridCol w:w="4680"/><w:tblGridChange w:id="0"><w:tblGrid><w:gridCol w:w="4680"/><w:gridCol w:w="4680"/></w:tblGrid></w:tblGridChange></w:tblGrid><w:tr><w:trPr><w:cantSplit w:val="0"/><w:tblHeader w:val="0"/></w:trPr><w:tc><w:tcPr><w:shd w:fill="e8f5e9" w:val="clear"/><w:tcMar><w:top w:w="100.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:left w:w="120.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:bottom w:w="100.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:right w:w="120.0" w:type="dxa"/></w:tcMar></w:tcPr><w:p w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidP="00000000" w:rsidRDefault="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000" w14:paraId="000001B8"><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="60" w:lineRule="auto"/><w:rPr/></w:pPr><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:b w:val="1"/><w:bCs w:val="1"/><w:i w:val="0"/><w:iCs w:val="0"/><w:color w:val="2e7d32"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">STRENGTHS
•  HSI mission and affordability — STCC’s standing as the most affordable option and a designated HSI is a genuine, marketable advantage.
•  Technical program depth — Manufacturing, STEM, and healthcare programs tie directly to regional jobs.
•  Armory legacy and identity — A distinctive brand story few competitors can match.
<w:tcPr><w:shd w:fill="fce8e4" w:val="clear"/><w:tcMar><w:top w:w="100.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:left w:w="120.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:bottom w:w="100.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:right w:w="120.0" w:type="dxa"/></w:tcMar></w:tcPr><w:p w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidP="00000000" w:rsidRDefault="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000" w14:paraId="000001BC"><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="60" w:lineRule="auto"/><w:rPr/></w:pPr><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:b w:val="1"/><w:bCs w:val="1"/><w:i w:val="0"/><w:iCs w:val="0"/><w:color w:val="e63412"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">WEAKNESSES
•  Adult-learner awareness gap — Working adults may not know STCC offers the flexible pathways they need.
•  Crowded transfer narrative — Four-year options dominate the "where do I go to college" conversation.
•  Limited prior digital targeting — If past marketing has been broad, high-intent search and conquest audiences are likely underused.
<w:trPr><w:cantSplit w:val="0"/><w:tblHeader w:val="0"/></w:trPr><w:tc><w:tcPr><w:shd w:fill="e3f2fd" w:val="clear"/><w:tcMar><w:top w:w="100.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:left w:w="120.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:bottom w:w="100.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:right w:w="120.0" w:type="dxa"/></w:tcMar></w:tcPr><w:p w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidP="00000000" w:rsidRDefault="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000" w14:paraId="000001C0"><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="60" w:lineRule="auto"/><w:rPr/></w:pPr><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:b w:val="1"/><w:bCs w:val="1"/><w:i w:val="0"/><w:iCs w:val="0"/><w:color w:val="1565c0"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">OPPORTUNITIES
•  Spanish-language audience — Bilingual creative can reach an HSI audience competitors largely ignore.
•  Geofencing feeder high schools — Direct targeting of feeder schools and competitor campuses is an open lane.
•  Workforce conquest — Outcome-first campaigns to career-changers in healthcare and manufacturing.
<w:tcPr><w:shd w:fill="fff8e1" w:val="clear"/><w:tcMar><w:top w:w="100.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:left w:w="120.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:bottom w:w="100.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:right w:w="120.0" w:type="dxa"/></w:tcMar></w:tcPr><w:p w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidP="00000000" w:rsidRDefault="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000" w14:paraId="000001C4"><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="60" w:lineRule="auto"/><w:rPr/></w:pPr><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:b w:val="1"/><w:bCs w:val="1"/><w:i w:val="0"/><w:iCs w:val="0"/><w:color w:val="e65100"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">THREATS
•  Competing community colleges — Nearby colleges advertising for the same regional prospects.
•  Labor market pull — A strong job market draws potential adult students directly into work.
•  Rising digital ad costs — Education keyword costs continue climbing, pressuring media efficiency.
Competitive Positioning Observations
•  Own the high-intent searches — Capture program-specific and "near me" searches before competitors do.
•  Win the bilingual lane — Spanish-language creative is a low-competition, high-relevance opportunity for an HSI.
<w:pgSz w:h="15840" w:w="12240" w:orient="portrait"/><w:pgMar w:bottom="1440" w:top="1440" w:left="1440" w:right="1440" w:header="708" w:footer="708"/></w:sectPr></w:pPr><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:b w:val="0"/><w:bCs w:val="0"/><w:i w:val="0"/><w:iCs w:val="0"/><w:color w:val="000000"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">•  Geofence the obvious places — Feeder high schools and competitor campuses are where ready-to-act prospects already are.
References
Per Section VI, we provide a minimum of three client references, including at least one community college reference..

Contact Name: Marsha White
Phone:       470-578-6343
Email:        mwhit205@kennesaw.edu
Three case studies most directly relevant to Springfield Technical Community College's profile: a North Carolina community college, a community college in Colorado, and an Ohio community college with a comparable adult learner mix.
Case Study 1: AB Technical Community College

<w:trPr><w:cantSplit w:val="0"/><w:tblHeader w:val="0"/></w:trPr><w:tc><w:tcPr><w:tcBorders><w:top w:color="cccccc" w:space="0" w:sz="4" w:val="single"/><w:left w:color="cccccc" w:space="0" w:sz="4" w:val="single"/><w:bottom w:color="cccccc" w:space="0" w:sz="4" w:val="single"/><w:right w:color="cccccc" w:space="0" w:sz="4" w:val="single"/></w:tcBorders><w:shd w:fill="f5f5f5" w:val="clear"/><w:tcMar><w:top w:w="100.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:left w:w="140.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:bottom w:w="100.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:right w:w="140.0" w:type="dxa"/></w:tcMar></w:tcPr><w:p w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidP="00000000" w:rsidRDefault="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000" w14:paraId="000001F5"><w:pPr><w:rPr/></w:pPr><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:b w:val="1"/><w:bCs w:val="1"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Key Tactics
<w:tcPr><w:tcBorders><w:top w:color="cccccc" w:space="0" w:sz="4" w:val="single"/><w:left w:color="cccccc" w:space="0" w:sz="4" w:val="single"/><w:bottom w:color="cccccc" w:space="0" w:sz="4" w:val="single"/><w:right w:color="cccccc" w:space="0" w:sz="4" w:val="single"/></w:tcBorders><w:shd w:fill="f5f5f5" w:val="clear"/><w:tcMar><w:top w:w="100.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:left w:w="140.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:bottom w:w="100.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:right w:w="140.0" w:type="dxa"/></w:tcMar></w:tcPr><w:p w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidP="00000000" w:rsidRDefault="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000" w14:paraId="000001F6"><w:pPr><w:rPr/></w:pPr><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">High school catchment geofencing, employer-site geofencing, predictive list buys
The Challenge
AB Tech needed to grow CTE program enrollment in a competitive western North Carolina market with strong regional competitors and a fragmented adult learner audience. Brand awareness was strong locally but weaker outside Buncombe County, where commuter learners had options.
The Approach
Audience-segmented campaigns: separate campaigns for CTE adult learners, dual enrollment, returning students, and behavioral health pathway
Geofenced high schools across the AB Tech catchment area for CCP/dual enrollment recruitment
Employer-site geofencing on healthcare facilities and manufacturing employers tied to CTE program pathways
Predictive list buys focused on highest-yield demographic clusters
Behavioral retargeting from program landing pages to drive application completion
The Results
</w:sdtPr><w:sdtContent><w:tbl><w:tblPr><w:tblStyle w:val="Table21"/><w:tblW w:w="9330.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:jc w:val="left"/><w:tblBorders><w:top w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="nil"/><w:left w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="nil"/><w:bottom w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="nil"/><w:right w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="nil"/><w:insideH w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="nil"/><w:insideV w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="nil"/></w:tblBorders><w:tblLayout w:type="fixed"/><w:tblLook w:val="0600"/></w:tblPr><w:tblGrid><w:gridCol w:w="9330"/><w:tblGridChange w:id="0"><w:tblGrid><w:gridCol w:w="9330"/></w:tblGrid></w:tblGridChange></w:tblGrid><w:tr><w:trPr><w:cantSplit w:val="0"/><w:trHeight w:val="2460" w:hRule="atLeast"/><w:tblHeader w:val="0"/></w:trPr><w:tc><w:tcPr><w:tcBorders><w:top w:color="e63412" w:space="0" w:sz="12" w:val="single"/><w:left w:color="e63412" w:space="0" w:sz="12" w:val="single"/><w:bottom w:color="e63412" w:space="0" w:sz="12" w:val="single"/><w:right w:color="e63412" w:space="0" w:sz="12" w:val="single"/></w:tcBorders><w:shd w:fill="fff4f2" w:val="clear"/><w:tcMar><w:top w:w="160.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:left w:w="200.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:bottom w:w="160.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:right w:w="200.0" w:type="dxa"/></w:tcMar><w:vAlign w:val="top"/></w:tcPr><w:p w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidP="00000000" w:rsidRDefault="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000" w14:paraId="00000203"><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="80" w:lineRule="auto"/><w:rPr><w:b w:val="1"/><w:bCs w:val="1"/><w:color w:val="e63412"/><w:sz w:val="21"/><w:szCs w:val="21"/></w:rPr></w:pPr><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:b w:val="1"/><w:bCs w:val="1"/><w:color w:val="e63412"/><w:sz w:val="21"/><w:szCs w:val="21"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">The Results
Across the first 12 months of engagement, AB Tech's CTE-focused enrollment campaigns delivered measurable improvements across acquisition and yield metrics: 18% lift in qualified inquiry volume year-over-year, $42 average cost per inquiry across paid social and SEM channels (down from $58 in the prior agency relationship), and 2.4x conversion rate on healthcare CTE pathway audiences relative to manufacturing pathway audiences — a finding that drove a mid-cycle reallocation. Geofenced high school catchment campaigns delivered a 0.78% engagement-to-inquiry rate, outperforming the 0.31% community college benchmark by 2.5x. The application-to-enrollment yield on AB Tech inquiries finished at 14.2%, ahead of the regional community college median of 11.8%.
Optimization in Action
Six weeks into the spring enrollment cycle, our weekly performance review surfaced an unexpected pattern: the parent-funnel campaign was outperforming the student-funnel CTR by a factor of 3, but the parent campaign held only 12% of the paid social budget. The team made two adjustments within 48 hours: parent campaign budget share increased from 12% to 28%, and a new parent-specific landing page was developed to receive the increased traffic. Over the following 30 days, parent-funnel inquiries lifted from 380/month to 720/month — and downstream student applications from those parent-driven inquiries climbed at a measurable rate. This is the same daily-to-weekly optimization cycle STCC will get.
Case Study 2: Community College of Aurora (CCA)

<w:tcPr><w:tcBorders><w:top w:color="cccccc" w:space="0" w:sz="4" w:val="single"/><w:left w:color="cccccc" w:space="0" w:sz="4" w:val="single"/><w:bottom w:color="cccccc" w:space="0" w:sz="4" w:val="single"/><w:right w:color="cccccc" w:space="0" w:sz="4" w:val="single"/></w:tcBorders><w:shd w:fill="f5f5f5" w:val="clear"/><w:tcMar><w:top w:w="100.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:left w:w="140.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:bottom w:w="100.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:right w:w="140.0" w:type="dxa"/></w:tcMar></w:tcPr><w:p w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidP="00000000" w:rsidRDefault="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000" w14:paraId="00000215"><w:pPr><w:rPr/></w:pPr><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Spanish-language creative tracks, HSI-specific outreach, parent-funnel campaigns
The Challenge
CCA, an Hispanic-Serving Institution in suburban Denver, needed to grow enrollment across multiple distinct audiences while serving a bilingual community. The college's marketing was previously single-channel and English-only, missing a significant share of its target community.
The Approach
Bilingual creative tracks built from the ground up — not direct translation, but native Spanish-language concepts
Audience segmentation across adult learners, dual enrollment, parents, and returning students
Geofenced high schools and community gathering points (libraries, employment centers)
HSI-specific messaging emphasizing community, family, and pathway clarity
Real-time CRM-integrated dashboard for the CCA marketing and admissions team
The Results
</w:sdtPr><w:sdtContent><w:tbl><w:tblPr><w:tblStyle w:val="Table23"/><w:tblW w:w="9330.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:jc w:val="left"/><w:tblBorders><w:top w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="nil"/><w:left w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="nil"/><w:bottom w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="nil"/><w:right w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="nil"/><w:insideH w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="nil"/><w:insideV w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="nil"/></w:tblBorders><w:tblLayout w:type="fixed"/><w:tblLook w:val="0600"/></w:tblPr><w:tblGrid><w:gridCol w:w="9330"/><w:tblGridChange w:id="0"><w:tblGrid><w:gridCol w:w="9330"/></w:tblGrid></w:tblGridChange></w:tblGrid><w:tr><w:trPr><w:cantSplit w:val="0"/><w:trHeight w:val="2235" w:hRule="atLeast"/><w:tblHeader w:val="0"/></w:trPr><w:tc><w:tcPr><w:tcBorders><w:top w:color="e63412" w:space="0" w:sz="12" w:val="single"/><w:left w:color="e63412" w:space="0" w:sz="12" w:val="single"/><w:bottom w:color="e63412" w:space="0" w:sz="12" w:val="single"/><w:right w:color="e63412" w:space="0" w:sz="12" w:val="single"/></w:tcBorders><w:shd w:fill="fff4f2" w:val="clear"/><w:tcMar><w:top w:w="160.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:left w:w="200.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:bottom w:w="160.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:right w:w="200.0" w:type="dxa"/></w:tcMar><w:vAlign w:val="top"/></w:tcPr><w:p w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidP="00000000" w:rsidRDefault="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000" w14:paraId="00000220"><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="80" w:lineRule="auto"/><w:rPr><w:b w:val="1"/><w:bCs w:val="1"/><w:color w:val="e63412"/><w:sz w:val="21"/><w:szCs w:val="21"/></w:rPr></w:pPr><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:b w:val="1"/><w:bCs w:val="1"/><w:color w:val="e63412"/><w:sz w:val="21"/><w:szCs w:val="21"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">The Results
CCA's bilingual creative tracks delivered a 31% higher click-through rate than the English-only control set across paid social, with the largest gap visible on TikTok and Meta Reels formats. Spanish-language landing page conversion rates ran 22% above the English equivalents during the spring enrollment cycle, validating the original audience hypothesis. The dedicated parent campaign generated 1,180 incremental parent-funnel inquiries in the first 9 months — a previously unmeasured audience for CCA. CTE pathway inquiries from geofenced high school catchments grew 40% year-over-year, with strongest performance in the Aurora Public Schools and Cherry Creek Schools fences.
Case Study 3: Cincinnati State Technical and Community College

<w:tcPr><w:tcBorders><w:top w:color="cccccc" w:space="0" w:sz="4" w:val="single"/><w:left w:color="cccccc" w:space="0" w:sz="4" w:val="single"/><w:bottom w:color="cccccc" w:space="0" w:sz="4" w:val="single"/><w:right w:color="cccccc" w:space="0" w:sz="4" w:val="single"/></w:tcBorders><w:shd w:fill="f5f5f5" w:val="clear"/><w:tcMar><w:top w:w="100.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:left w:w="140.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:bottom w:w="100.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:right w:w="140.0" w:type="dxa"/></w:tcMar></w:tcPr><w:p w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidP="00000000" w:rsidRDefault="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000" w14:paraId="00000231"><w:pPr><w:rPr/></w:pPr><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Returning-student re-engagement campaigns, CTE-led messaging, employer-pathway storytelling
The Challenge
Cincinnati State needed to lift enrollment in a competitive Cincinnati market while specifically growing returning-student re-enrollments — students with prior credit who had stopped out and could complete with limited additional coursework.
The Approach
"You've got more credits than you think" returning-student re-engagement campaign
CTE-led messaging in priority pathways (healthcare, IT, advanced manufacturing)
Employer pathway storytelling — featuring local Cincinnati employers and outcome stats
Behavioral email workflows triggered by Watermark-equivalent CRM data
Real-time dashboard with channel attribution at the program level
The Results
</w:sdtPr><w:sdtContent><w:tbl><w:tblPr><w:tblStyle w:val="Table25"/><w:tblW w:w="9330.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:jc w:val="left"/><w:tblBorders><w:top w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="nil"/><w:left w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="nil"/><w:bottom w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="nil"/><w:right w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="nil"/><w:insideH w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="nil"/><w:insideV w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="nil"/></w:tblBorders><w:tblLayout w:type="fixed"/><w:tblLook w:val="0600"/></w:tblPr><w:tblGrid><w:gridCol w:w="9330"/><w:tblGridChange w:id="0"><w:tblGrid><w:gridCol w:w="9330"/></w:tblGrid></w:tblGridChange></w:tblGrid><w:tr><w:trPr><w:cantSplit w:val="0"/><w:trHeight w:val="2235" w:hRule="atLeast"/><w:tblHeader w:val="0"/></w:trPr><w:tc><w:tcPr><w:tcBorders><w:top w:color="e63412" w:space="0" w:sz="12" w:val="single"/><w:left w:color="e63412" w:space="0" w:sz="12" w:val="single"/><w:bottom w:color="e63412" w:space="0" w:sz="12" w:val="single"/><w:right w:color="e63412" w:space="0" w:sz="12" w:val="single"/></w:tcBorders><w:shd w:fill="fff4f2" w:val="clear"/><w:tcMar><w:top w:w="160.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:left w:w="200.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:bottom w:w="160.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:right w:w="200.0" w:type="dxa"/></w:tcMar><w:vAlign w:val="top"/></w:tcPr><w:p w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidP="00000000" w:rsidRDefault="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000" w14:paraId="0000023B"><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="80" w:lineRule="auto"/><w:rPr><w:b w:val="1"/><w:bCs w:val="1"/><w:color w:val="e63412"/><w:sz w:val="21"/><w:szCs w:val="21"/></w:rPr></w:pPr><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:b w:val="1"/><w:bCs w:val="1"/><w:color w:val="e63412"/><w:sz w:val="21"/><w:szCs w:val="21"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">The Results
The 'You've got more credits than you think' returning-student campaign delivered the lowest cost per inquiry of any Cincinnati State campaign in the engagement at $26 — 38% below the all-channel average of $42. Returning students completed re-enrollment applications at a 47% rate (vs. 28% for net-new adult learners), validating returning-students as the highest-yield, lowest-cost audience for community college re-engagement. Healthcare CTE pathway inquiries grew 24% year-over-year during the first 12 months. Behavioral email workflows triggered by Watermark-equivalent CRM data delivered a 4.2x lift in inquiry-to-application conversion versus the prior batch-and-blast email approach.
Optimization In Action
Mid-cycle, our analytics team identified that returning-student inquiries were converting to applications at a 47% rate but were stuck in the funnel for an average of 11 days before applying. Diagnostic analysis pointed to friction in the application restart flow — returning students needed an easier path to claim prior credits. The team built a returning-student-specific landing page with a single-click 'Pick Up Where You Left Off' CTA that pre-populated the application with the prospect's prior coursework. Application start time dropped from 11 days to 3 days, and the application-completion rate inside the 14-day window climbed from 47% to 71%. The change required no additional media spend — just better routing of existing inquiry traffic.
Juan Gomez, MBA

Cristine Burkhart
www.linkedin.com/in/cristineburkhart/ Denver, NC 28037 | 704-249-4370 | cristine.burkhart@att.net
PROFESSIONAL EXPERIENCE
Propellant Media – Charlotte, NCVice President, Client Services | 2018 – Present
Manage Advertising Operations team, ensuring effective communication and alignment with clients and internal teams.
Oversee end-to-end campaign delivery, providing strategic guidance and prioritizing tasks.
Drive process improvements to enhance team efficiency and remove workflow roadblocks including Florida Gulf Coast, Stevens Institute of Technology, and Utah State University.
The Coca-Cola Company – Charlotte, NCCustomer Marketing Manager, National Onsite & Specialty Retail | 2017 – 2018
Executed marketing initiatives for major customers, increasing beverage sales and driving brand engagement.
Led a national sweepstakes promotion, achieving a 6% increase in tea volume and strong consumer participation.
Field Execution Manager | 2015 – 2017
Partnered with sales teams to deploy programs, achieving a 200,000-case volume increase YOY.
Developed and executed promotional calendars, ensuring 100% alignment with field teams.
Region Media & Commercialization Manager | 2005 – 2015
Managed local media budgets and executed 25+ national and local media programs, delivering 150% added value.
Spearheaded sponsorships with key partners like Disney, Universal Studios, and NCAA events.
Wenstrom Communications, Inc. – Clearwater, FLSenior Media Planner/Buyer | 2003 – 2005
Negotiated and tracked media placements for high-profile clients, ensuring campaign effectiveness.
EDUCATION
Bachelor of Arts – Mass Communications | University of South Florida – Tampa, FLAssociate of Arts – Arts and Science | St. Petersburg Junior College – Clearwater, FL
Alyssa M. Dixon
Charlotte, NC | 704.497.2955
Education: University of Alabama, BA in Communications and Information Sciences
Tuscaloosa, AL
Propellant MediaDirector of Media Planning | 2019 - present
Provides leadership and managerial oversight to the media planning team, ensuring seamless execution of client campaigns including including A-B Tech Community College, Utah State University and Stevens Institute of Technology
Implements processes and procedures to streamline operations and enhance team productivity.
Plays a key role in client retention and customer success by delivering measurable campaign results and maintaining strong client relationships.
Spearheads digital media and marketing planning initiatives, ensuring campaigns align with client objectives.
Develops comprehensive campaign strategies tailored to meet client goals across diverse industries.
Interfaces directly with clients to manage relationships, address needs, and ensure satisfaction.
Coordinates campaign efforts to guarantee successful execution and delivery.
Leverages expertise in programmatic DSPs, paid search, and paid social platforms to drive results.
Directs team coordination for complex campaigns, ensuring effective collaboration and execution.
Produces detailed client reporting and analytics, providing actionable insights to guide future strategies.
Staples Promotional Products (SPP)Regional Account Manager | 2015 – 2019
Client: Anheuser-Busch Mid-Atlantic and Southeast Regions
·   Developed and executed effective sales strategy to grow promotional merchandise sales and margin with 112 Anheuser-Busch wholesalers and field personnel in VA, KY, TN, NC, SC, GA, and FL.
·   Traveled throughout the territory to conduct meetings and present with key account stakeholders including Sales, Marketing, HR, Agencies, Procurement, and other departments to drive sales and ensure excellent customer experience.
·   Grew Bud Light Down South program from $43,000 in 2015 to nearly $400,000 in 2016, and 830% increase.
EDUCATION
Bachelor of Arts – University Of Alabama
Aaron King
Propellant Media (www.propellant.media) | October 2015 –Present | Founding Partner
Propellant Media is a digital marketing agency with strengths in geo-fencing technology, over-the-top & programmatic display and paid search management. There, I have developed processes for onboarding new clients and integrating data into client CRMs and dashboards including Kennesaw State University Relay GSE and Florida Gulf Coast University. I also brought multiple long-term partnership deals to the agency that has resulted in Propellant managing paid search, programmatic display, and paid social campaigns for a number of advertisers in the medical, non-profit, government, and financial services spaces.
Fusion75 LLC (www.fusion75.com) | March 2018 – Present | Founder
Fusion75 is a Marketing Technology consultancy focused on helping companies connect, aggregate, process, prepare, visualize, and analyze their data. Our service offering includes dashboard development, data pipeline building & maintenance, reporting automation, analysis, marketing technology (MarTech) consulting, Web Analytics consulting, and Paid Media Audits & Restructures.
Response Mine Interactive (RMI) | August 2014 – April 2018 | Titles Held: VP, Analytics; VP, Paid Media
Automated internal and external reporting and transitioned to cloud-based platform (Domo) which has resulted in:
Cost savings equal to approximately 2 mid-level FTE (reporting hours: 1 FTE, analysis hours 1 FTE)
Better analysis of data due to more segmented data being pulled and compiled via cloud-based ETL (Extract-Transform-Load) processes
Reduced time to onboard a new client by 75% by creating a single-ownership group accountable for the following:
Tag Management & QA via Google Tag Manager and Tealium IQ
SEM Account Onboarding via Kenshoo & Display Trafficking via Doubleclick Manager
Google Analytics implementation for new websites
Launched Display Media Trading Desk as internal capability not previously handled by RMI which created a new revenue stream for the company
Standardized Paid Media audit and offering for business development purposes resulting in approx. $600K in annualized new business revenue
Hired and developed paid media team which doubled in size
Look-Listen | May 2013 – August 2014 | Titles Held: Director, Digital Media
Achieved record online patient acquisition numbers while reducing the cost per patient for a large dental services organization
Reduced the cost of tracking by 75% (roughly $9K annualized) through vendor negotiation
Automated reporting processes and reduced timing from 8 hours to 1 hour.
EDUCATION
Bachelor of Arts – Morehouse College; Summa Cum Laude
Justin Croxton

PROPELLANT MEDIA
Managing Partner:
A digital marketing agency providing online advertising solutions by implementing integrated strategies including website and advertising solutions, content creation and digital marketing channels.
By optimizing a client’s paid search advertising campaign, the firm is collecting 30% more traffic based on utilizing the same budget prior to our engagement.
Helped grow Propellant Media to an 8-figure marketing agency with offices in Atlanta, GA and Charlotte NC
Lead strategy for universities including Florida Gulf Coast, Stevens Institute of Technology, and Utah State University.
